## Recorded progress and status

The available records show both ‘Current’ and ‘Lapsed’ labels. For example, Seanad Electoral (University Members) (Amendment) Bill 2020 and Protection of Private Residences (Against Targeted Picketing) Bill 2021 are marked ‘Current’, while Health Insurance (International Students) (Amendment) Bill 2021, Education (Relationships and Sexuality Education) (Amendment) Bill 2022 and Protection of Retail Workers Bill 2024 are marked ‘Lapsed’. These labels matter because they record status, not a certified final outcome; the records also do not establish that the activity shown is the latest. [a-0918fa4cd253aa392504]
_Provisional interpretation — moderate qualitative confidence (provisional)_

The records show substantial stage activity for Protection of Private Residences (Against Targeted Picketing) Bill 2021, including Committee Stage on 23 May 2024 and Report Stage and Fifth Stage entries on 4 July 2024. For that Bill, a Bill Lapsed Event and Second Stage are both recorded on 8 November 2024, with another Second Stage entry on 1 May 2025; the Bill is also marked ‘Current’. Seanad Electoral (University Members) (Amendment) Bill 2020 is likewise marked ‘Current’, while a Bill Lapsed Event and Committee Stage are recorded on 29 January 2025 and a further Committee Stage on 27 March 2025. The records establish these entries but do not reconcile them, explain why they appear together or certify either Bill’s final outcome. [a-703304440289d0884464]
_Provisional interpretation — moderate qualitative confidence (provisional)_

More recent examples include Second Stage entries for Road Traffic (Amendment) (Jax’s Law) Bill on 16 September 2026, Public Health (Tobacco) (Amendment) (Smokefree Generation) Bill on 22 September 2026, and Artificial Intelligence Companion Services (Protection of Children) Bill on 29 September 2026. Each is marked ‘Current’ in the available records. These entries show recorded activity on those dates; they do not establish what happened afterwards or the Bills’ final outcomes. ## Limitations and unanswered questions

- These are findings from the available parliamentary records, not a certified complete account.
- The source's last update and completeness are not established. Retrieval time is not source freshness.
- Recorded Bill statuses and dated activities do not by themselves establish a final outcome.
- Representation in separate Dáil terms does not establish continuous service or other parliamentary roles.
- This is an editorial request; the revision should condense the overview without removing the Bill findings or their evidence.
- The existing evidence supports explaining that recorded labels and events do not certify final outcomes, and that some entries are not reconciled. It does not establish outcomes beyond those records.
- More than one Bill in the existing findings has a recorded lapse event and a later stage entry; the evidence can be shown for the examples already identified, but the intended Bill is unclear.
- The available career lookup does not establish parliamentary offices, committee roles or other parliamentary roles; no role-discovery tool is available.
- The existing Bill research can be retained, and the career lookup can support a separate section on recorded constituencies and Dáil-term identities. The requested parliamentary-role details remain unavailable through the available tools.
- Whether the recorded lifecycle information is complete and current as of the reporting date, and what the certified final outcome of each Bill was.
- Why some Bills have a recorded ‘Current’ status alongside a Bill Lapsed Event and later stage entries; the intended Bill in the earlier follow-up is also unclear because this pattern appears for more than one Bill.
- Mary Lou McDonald’s parliamentary offices, committee roles, other parliamentary roles and service dates, and whether her service was continuous.
